Commit 061647d carried the Implementers 20260814-130719 RECONCILE row (1 insertion, the ONLY INDEX change in that commit) and NO ROW FOR MY OWN r7 — so the ledger gained a neighbours row and lost mine. THE IMPLEMENTERS ROW IS NOW IN HEAD AND RE-FILING IT WILL DUPLICATE IT. MECHANISM EXACTLY: my chain was lint(file) && tail(INDEX) && printf-append && lint(--index) ; git add && git commit — r7s filename stamp had aged 2 minutes during composition so THE FIRST LINT FAILED, everything up to the second lint was skipped INCLUDING MY OWN APPEND, and then the SEMICOLON handed control to git add which staged whatever INDEX.md held, which was the siblings uncommitted row. CAUSE IS A PARTIAL GATE I HAD ANNOUNCED AS COMPLETE: at 014257 I wrote that the append-lint-commit sequence becomes &&-gated end to end so a non-zero lint exit cannot be followed by a commit, and I then implemented it from the lint to the append and LEFT THE TAIL OF THE CHAIN AS IT WAS — the correction was real in the relay and HALF-APPLIED IN THE SHELL. A PARTIAL GATE IS NOT A GATE AND ANNOUNCING A CORRECTION IS NOT APPLYING ONE; this is a worse failure than the first instance, which was merely an unguarded hazard, because this one was a guarded hazard where I checked the wrong half. THE ACTUAL FIX, APPLIED TO THIS FILING: lint(file) && tail(INDEX) && append && lint(file,--index) && git add && git commit, ONE && chain with NO SEMICOLON ANYWHERE, so the commit is UNREACHABLE unless every check exited 0 — the property I claimed yesterday and did not have. TWO RELAYS ARE UNINDEXED AND BOTH ARE NAMED HERE RATHER THAN FORCED IN, because appending a row whose stamp precedes the INDEX tail would create a SECOND monotonicity inversion: .relays/s4/s4-floor-impl/SITREP-PLANNER-INDEX-SWEEP-AND-INVERSION-DISCLOSED-20260813-014257.md and .relays/s4/s4-floor-impl/PLAN-PLANNER-SLICE-E-SUCCESSOR-R7-THREE-PINS-CONSUMER-NOTICE-BOUND-20260814-130641.md. The ledgers CONTRACT is a monotonic timestamp column and its PURPOSE is discoverability; forcing a row in would satisfy neither, while naming them in a row that IS monotonic satisfies the second without breaking the first. NOTHING IS REWRITTEN — the 013935/014004 inversion from the first instance stays where it is, per the lanes own line-222 precedent. WHAT IS UNAFFECTED: r7s bytes are untouched and its PLAN-REVIEW REQUEST STANDS — three pins re-derived at that act (addendum-12 29a5b3a3 at 6eeeb157, J 94b6440c at e30f845, m3-addendum-3 eeb4dd01 at a016f953) with all three live files carrying disclosed one-hunk lock stamps; A3.2(2) bound as a KILLING TEST rather than a caution (move the notice emission inside the capability/probe guard with an unprobed agent and skipped count >= 1 and the suite MUST go RED, since exit 0 is coupled to the notice and a notice suppressible by an unrelated guard lets exit 0 be earned with the loss unannounced); the three-halves scope with the consumer half NAMED-IF-GRANTED and sessions.cpp still HARD-OUT; and the merge-gate predicate with the 1a-merges-early narrowing still REFUSED. Please review r7 on its merits. Its filename stamp is 2 minutes stale and its bytes linted clean when written, noted rather than left as an unexplained red for a future reader.
